دفتر

A notebook you and your agents keep — and you can read it without them.

Daftar (دفتر) is the Persian word for a notebook. This one is kept by you and your AI agents, about the things you work on together, and you can read it without them.

What it does, in one example

  1. You, to your agent

    Ali and I bought a camera. She paid for it, ninety. I use it more, so we said I'd carry two thirds.

  2. Your agent

    Written down as one agreement: what was paid, by whom, who bears what — and that you said so, and when. The notebook's check passed, and its journal says what I did and why.

  3. Weeks later — another agent, of another make, with no memory of the first.
  4. You

    What do I owe Ali, and how do we know?

  5. The other agent

    You owe Ali 60 — read just now from the agreement, not from a stored balance. You said so yourself: the agreement records who said it and when, and the journal who wrote it down.

Nothing was lost between the two sessions, nothing was invented, and you did not have to explain it again. The whole case, with what the tools printed.

You talk; your agents keep it

You never have to open the notebook. Your agents write in it, run its tools, and answer from it — whichever agents you use, one after another or side by side. You can read every page of it yourself, and write in it when you need to, by the same rules: the gate treats a person and an agent alike.

It is like the project files an AI app keeps for you, with three differences: every fact says who said it and how they know; every change is written in a journal; and a gate refuses what breaks the rules. And it is yours — plain text in a private git repository, on your own computer. It works offline, and a small model running on an ordinary computer can keep it too.

Three rules

daftar is a notebook with three rules:

  1. Every fact says who said it and how they know — you, or the agent, and whether it was told, measured, or guessed.
  2. Every change is written in a journal, in the same commit — what was done and why.
  3. A gate refuses any change that breaks a rule — whether a person or an agent made it.

The notebook is plain text in a private git repository: a garden of beans, one file for each thing it keeps. People and agents of any make write there in one language, and the gate reads every commit before it is recorded.

The promise

Whatever agent wrote it, a fact here says who said it and how they know, the change that put it there says why, and you can read it without the agent.

Start, in three steps

  1. Tell your agent to read INSTALL.md

    You need a coding agent with a shell and git, and a place for a private repository. Tell the agent:

    Read https://raw.githubusercontent.com/0mit/daftar/master/INSTALL.md and follow it. My notebook goes at ~/garden-sam; my private remote is git@github.com:me/garden.git.

  2. It grows your garden, with you as its gardener

    Your own person bean is the first thing in it, and the gate's last line names you. This is the command it runs, and the lines that matter in what it prints:

    ~/daftar$ python3 seed/germinate.py ~/garden-sam --gardener sam --gardener-name "Sam"
garden-sam (daftar v0.42.0, gardener sam, garden 00479fa7a243): 1 docs, 0 error(s), 0 warning(s)
…
germinated: ~/garden-sam  (std-vocab@26.0, daftar v0.42.0)
…
The garden passes its own gate. Its gardener, sam, is planted: the person who keeps it, named in GARDEN.md.
…

  3. Next time, tell it to read AGENTS.md in the garden

    (In Claude Code: “load daftar”.) The agent reads AGENTS.md in the garden — a reading order, not a set of rules — and runs the gate. From then on you talk about your things, and the agent keeps the notebook. An assistant in a chat window, with no shell, cannot run the gate: paste it seed/WELCOME.md, and what it gives back is a proposal for you, or an agent with a shell, to commit.

INSTALL.md is written for the agent that will grow the garden, and has the by-hand path for a person without one.

Who it is for

It is not for everyone. If one careful person keeps a few notes for one agent, this is more than you need. It is for people who run several agents, of several makes, across many sessions, on one set of real things — and who need what those agents leave behind to be true, attributed, and mergeable; and for anyone who will one day hand that set of things to someone else.

The words you will meet

Bean, garden, gardener, gate, journal, anchor, crown, proposal, peering, and the rest
wordmeaning
beanone managed thing, as one Markdown file in beans/ — facts in the front matter, prose below
gardena git repository of beans: one notebook. Private to whoever keeps it
gardenerthe person — or organisation — who keeps a garden, named in its GARDEN.md: the first bean of a garden grown with --gardener. Agents tend a garden; its gardener keeps it and ratifies what an agent may not decide
seedseed/: the kit a new garden is grown from — the vocabulary, the templates, germinate.py
vocabularythe rules, as data: seed/std-vocab.md for every garden, plus a garden's own VOCAB.md
gatebin/dmcheck.py, run as a pre-commit hook: a commit that breaks a rule is refused
journallog/journal.md: every change, who made it and why. The gate refuses an unrecorded change
anchora fact that identifies an object (a serial, a domain name), so two gardens recognise the same thing
nature / genoswhat sort of being it is: soma, a body; lekton, what exists by being said and agreed; or empsychon, what lives, while it lives — refined by a genos, its kind, such as host. The words are Greek, and MODEL.md gives each
facetan aspect of ownership, e.g. legal or technical; each has exactly one owner, and one entry saying who answers for it
crownwhere every ownership chain ends: at theos, which no bean names, through a branch for each nature — physis for soma, logos for lekton, agape, love that does not possess, for empsychon. A person writes owned_by: { legal: { crown: agape } }
profilean opt-in group of rules, e.g. domain, for gardens that hold that kind of thing
vacancya value the vocabulary offers that nothing uses yet, stated with a reason
Contract of PartsMODEL.md: which decisions an agent may take alone and which a person must ratify
proposalwhat one garden offers another: one file of beans, laid outside both gardens, which the other garden's gardener takes in by committing it — or does not. Taking it in accepts nothing on the gardener's behalf. bin/dmpropose.py
peeringhow gardens meet: as peers, each configured at its own end, through the agreements between their gardeners and the proposals made under them, in the language they share. Owned by no garden

The table is README.md's, at the release these pages document.

Use cases

You do not work in the notebook: you talk to your agents, in your own words, and they keep it. Each case below starts there — what a person says and what they read back — and then shows what happens underneath, for anyone who wants to know. They happen in two demo gardens: garden-sam, kept by Sam, and garden-ali, kept by Ali, who shares costs with him and keeps a notebook of her own.

  • Your machines, and the domain they answer on

    A NAS at home serves a website on a registered domain; a server is rented beside it. You say what you run, and are told in time what is running out.

    dmcursor, dmstale

  • Money between two people

    A camera shared, a loan agreed over dinner and repaid monthly, and the bank statement that proves it. Who owes whom is read, never written.

    dmledger, dmstale, dmcal, dmsafe

  • Two people's notebooks meet

    Neither agent writes in the other's notebook: a proposal crosses, is read and taken, and Ali writes her own yes. And first, a rehearsal that is never taken for fact.

    dmpropose

  • Several agents, one notebook

    A coding agent, an assistant in a chat window, a small model on your own computer: none remembers the others, and the gate judges them all alike.

    dmcheck, dmpropose

Four ways to read a case

Each case can be read by four people, the way the machinery is drawn at four levels — each for someone with a different question. The dial at the top of the page chooses: the whole page, every case in it, reads as that person would, and goes deeper as you turn it.

  • The person (Orient) — what do I say, and what do I get back? The conversation, in plain words.
  • The curious (Understand) — what does the agent do with my words? Each tool it calls, why, and what the tool answered.
  • The keeper (Operate) — is it working? The tools' own output, whole, and what a refusal says.
  • The auditor (Inspect) — what exactly was written? Every file, as it stands.

The conversations are written for these pages. Everything the agents report in them — every figure, date and refusal — is what the tools print, and that output is real.

Starting

Tell your agent to read INSTALL.md and follow it: it grows your notebook, with you as its gardener. On a Windows computer the same holds, with three differences the agent has to know — Starting on Windows.

How these pages are made

  • site/build.py clones daftar at v0.42.0 and grows the two gardens with seed/germinate.py.
  • It commits the recipes of seed/COOKBOOK.md one at a time, and runs the commands shown. What each block shows below a command is what that command printed. A line … marks lines left out; no line is edited.
  • The clock is held at 2026-10-27 09:00 UTC, so a date the tools count to reads the same on every build. The day comes after what the cookbook's story tells, whose loan is first paid on 1 October 2026, even where that is later than the release.
  • A garden's id differs in every build, because germination writes a random seed into the first commit; so does every fingerprint and name that carries one. They are marked with a dotted underline.
  • The directory that holds the gardens is shown as ~. Inputs the build wrote, such as a block handed to dmsafe, are shown as inputs.

Your situation is not here? Describe it in your own words — the form asks for the situation, never for anything from your garden.

Your machines, and the domain they answer on

Sam keeps a NAS at home that serves his website, has registered example.org for it, and rents a virtual server. Each of these is one bean: what it is, what identifies it, who said so, who owns it and who answers for it. The domain's registration runs out on a date, and a tool reads that date before it does.

garden-sam, daftar v0.42.0, the day held at 2026-10-27

What Sam says, and what he reads

Orient — for the person. Sam tells his agent what he runs, and asks to be warned in time. The conversation is written for this page; everything the agent reports in it is what the tools print under Operate.

  1. Sam · typed

    My website runs on the NAS at home, on example.org. I also rent a small server somewhere. Keep track of all of it, and tell me before the domain runs out.

  2. Sam's agent

    Which web server runs the site on the NAS? And who is the domain registered with — do you know when it expires, and whether it renews itself?

  3. Sam

    nginx. Example Registrar; it runs to the middle of January, and auto-renew is on.

  4. Sam's agent

    Five pages, one for each thing: the domain, the NAS, the website that lives on it, nginx — software owned by its own project, not by you — and the rented server. Each says who owns it, who answers for it, and that you are the one who said so. The notebook's check passed each time.

    One warning already: example.org's registration expires on 15 January 2027 — in 80 days. Auto-renew is on, so this is a reminder, not an alarm; I will say it again whenever you ask what needs attention.

  5. Sam

    If I ever move the site off the NAS, what breaks?

  6. Sam's agent

    Before touching the website I look it up first: it lives in the NAS, and nothing is recorded that must be attended to before it is touched. The NAS is where I would start.

What the agent does underneath

Understand — for the curious. The notebook's own tools, called by the agent, one after another. Each step in plain words first, then exactly, with what the tool printed.

  1. write It writes one page for each thing

    The domain, the NAS, the website, nginx and the rented server are five files, each small enough to read at a glance.

    Exactly: beans/example-org.md, nas.md, website.md, nginx.md and vps-a.md, in the shapes of the notebook's recipes, committed in four steps. The website names the NAS it lives in. They are shown whole under Inspect.

  2. dmjournal It says what it did

    Each change gets a line in the journal, naming the pages it touched.

    ~/garden-sam$ python3 bin/dmjournal.py sam "nginx, and the website it serves" --body "- action: added [[nginx]] and [[website]], from the cookbook."
## 2026-10-27 09:00+00:00 · sam · nginx, and the website it serves
  3. git commit It saves — and the gate checks first

    Saving runs the gate over the whole notebook. Here it also checks that every page says both who owns it and who answers for it, and that every chain of ownership ends somewhere. When all is well it says so in one line.

    ~/garden-sam$ git commit -qm "nginx, and the website it serves"
garden-sam (daftar v0.42.0, gardener sam, garden 00479fa7a243): 5 docs, 0 error(s), 0 warning(s)
fast: 14/14 corpus checks passed
  4. dmcursor Before touching a page, it asks what it stands on

    The agent looks a thing up before it changes it: what it lives in, what must be attended to.

    ~/garden-sam$ python3 bin/dmcursor.py website
cursor -> website   instance · empsychon · living in nas
  website — nginx on the NAS, serving example.org
…
  5. dmstale It reads what is running out

    Dates that matter — a registration, a certificate, a payment due — are read on every check, not remembered.

    ~/garden-sam$ python3 bin/dmstale.py
EXPIRING example-org.registration  expires 2027-01-15 (80 days)  auto_renew=enabled  registrar=Example Registrar Inc.  <-- an unrenewed name takes its DNS and its mail with it
…
expiring: 0 ok, 1 within their horizon, 0 EXPIRED
…
exit 1

What the tools say

Operate — for the keeper. The same tools, whole: what anyone who keeps this notebook sees when they run them, and what they do when one refuses.

Each recipe is committed with its journal entry. git commit runs the gate as a pre-commit hook, then the corpus checks every garden's hook runs:

~/garden-sam$ python3 bin/dmjournal.py sam "nginx, and the website it serves" --body "- action: added [[nginx]] and [[website]], from the cookbook."
## 2026-10-27 09:00+00:00 · sam · nginx, and the website it serves
~/garden-sam$ git add -A
~/garden-sam$ git commit -qm "nginx, and the website it serves"
garden-sam (daftar v0.42.0, gardener sam, garden 00479fa7a243): 5 docs, 0 error(s), 0 warning(s)
fast: 14/14 corpus checks passed

Before an agent touches the website, dmcursor says what it is and where it lives, and what must be attended to first:

~/garden-sam$ python3 bin/dmcursor.py website
cursor -> website   instance · empsychon · living in nas
  website — nginx on the NAS, serving example.org

MEASURED  — what is already known, and whether it still holds
  cache  none — this being has no recorded analysis; reading is unavoidable

ATTENTION — required before touching it
  none recorded on this being

INHERITED — along every chain the vocabulary declares acyclic
  nothing constraining inherited from any chain

dmstale reads the registration's expiry date and warns inside 90 days. It exits 1 when something needs action:

~/garden-sam$ python3 bin/dmstale.py
EXPIRING example-org.registration  expires 2027-01-15 (80 days)  auto_renew=enabled  registrar=Example Registrar Inc.  <-- an unrenewed name takes its DNS and its mail with it

expiring: 0 ok, 1 within their horizon, 0 EXPIRED

analysis_cache: 0 fresh, 0 stale, 0 not on this host, 0 unknown
exit 1

The gate's last line names the garden, the release it runs, its gardener and its id:

~/garden-sam$ python3 bin/dmcheck.py --all
garden-sam (daftar v0.42.0, gardener sam, garden 00479fa7a243): 6 docs, 0 error(s), 0 warning(s)

What the notebook records

Inspect — for the auditor. Exactly what was written, and why it is shaped so.

Every bean says what sort of being it records, in two words the law takes from Greek. Its nature is one of three: soma, a body — a being with extension in space, as the NAS is; lekton, the sayable — a being that exists by being said and agreed, as a domain is, or a program's code; or empsychon, the ensouled — a being that strives to persist as itself while it is alive: a person, or a program while it runs. Its genos, a kind, refines the nature: host, domain, instance. Each word, with its Greek.

A domain is registered for a term, not owned outright: the registry is its external owner, and Sam answers for it. Its registration belongs to the domain profile, which the garden opts into in its own VOCAB.md — a change to the garden's law, journalled as a RULE-CHANGE:

extends_profiles: [domain]
beans/example-org.md---
bean: example-org
genos: domain
title: "example.org — Sam's domain"
status: active
summary: "The domain Sam's website answers on."
nature: lekton
identity:
  status: confirmed
  anchors:
    - { key: fqdn, value: "example.org", class: logical, establishing: true }
provenance: { src: observed, by: "sam", as_of: 2026-10-27 }
owned_by: { legal: { external: "the .org registry, under a registration agreement" } }
responsibility: { legal: { holder: { bean: sam } } }
registration:
  registrar: "Example Registrar Inc."
  created: 2020-01-15
  expires: 2027-01-15
  auto_renew: enabled
  observed: 2026-10-27
  source: "WHOIS for example.org"
---
Sam's domain.

The NAS is a host, of the nature soma — a body — identified by the serial on its sticker; its host name only corroborates. provides_habitat is what lets a running thing live on it:

beans/nas.md---
bean: nas
genos: host
title: "nas — Sam's home NAS"
status: active
summary: "A NAS at home: file storage, and the web server for example.org."
nature: soma
identity:
  status: confirmed
  anchors:
    - { key: serial, value: "NAS-0042", class: hardware, establishing: true }
    - { key: hostname, value: "nas", class: network, establishing: false }
provenance: { src: observed, by: "sam", as_of: 2026-10-27 }
owned_by: { legal: { owner: { bean: sam } }, technical: { owner: { bean: sam } } }
responsibility: { legal: { holder: { bean: sam } }, technical: { holder: { bean: sam } } }
provides_habitat: linux-baremetal
roles:
  - { role: file-server }
  - { role: web }
---
The NAS.

The website is an instance: a running copy of nginx that lives on the NAS and is Sam's — of the nature empsychon, because it exists while it runs. “Serves this domain” has no relation of its own, so it is a refs entry with a rel:

beans/website.md---
bean: website
genos: instance
title: "website — nginx on the NAS, serving example.org"
status: active
summary: "The web server instance on the NAS that answers for example.org."
nature: empsychon
identity:
  status: confirmed
  anchors:
    - { key: identifier, value: "nginx:example.org@nas", class: logical, establishing: true }
provenance: { src: observed, by: "sam", as_of: 2026-10-27 }
instance_of: { bean: nginx }
lives_in: { bean: nas }
owned_by: { legal: { owner: { bean: sam } } }
responsibility: { legal: { holder: { bean: sam } } }
refs:
  domain: { bean: example-org, rel: serves }
---
The website.
The software it runs, and the rented server

nginx is a product owned by its project. The rented server is a virtual-host: it has no matter of its own, so it is identified by its name, and the provider owns it. It is empsychon too, not soma: it has no body, exists while it runs, and ends when it is torn down.

beans/nginx.md---
bean: nginx
genos: product
title: "nginx — the web server software"
status: active
summary: "Third-party web server software, run here but owned by its project."
nature: lekton
identity:
  status: confirmed
  anchors:
    - { key: identifier, value: "product:nginx", class: logical, establishing: true }
provenance: { src: observed, by: "sam", as_of: 2026-10-27 }
owned_by: { legal: { external: "the nginx project" } }
responsibility: { legal: { holder: { bean: sam } } }
---
The web server software.
beans/vps-a.md---
bean: vps-a
genos: virtual-host
title: "vps-a — a rented virtual server"
status: active
summary: "A VPS rented from a hosting provider."
nature: empsychon
identity:
  status: confirmed
  anchors:
    - { key: fqdn, value: "vps-a.example.org", class: logical, establishing: true }
provenance: { src: observed, by: "sam", as_of: 2026-10-27 }
owned_by: { legal: { external: "the hosting provider, which owns and operates the machine" } }
responsibility: { legal: { holder: { bean: sam } } }
provides_habitat: linux-vm
---
The rented server.

What it shows

  • Identity is by anchor, not by file name. A serial for the NAS, a domain name for the domain and for the rented server: two notebooks that never met recognise the same thing by it.
  • Ownership and habitat are separate. The website is Sam's, and lives in the NAS; moving it to another machine changes where it lives, never who owns it. Ownership rises.
  • A date that runs out is read by a tool, not by the gate. A check whose result changed with the calendar would make the gate refuse on some day for no committed reason; dmstale says so in its header.

Read more

  • The cookbook: a registered domain, a machine at home, software and a running copy of it, a rented VPS.
  • The model: identity and ownership.
  • The tools: bin/dmstale.py and bin/dmcursor.py, each explained in its first lines.

Is your situation like this, but not quite? Tell us how it differs.

Money between two people

Sam and Ali share a camera, and over dinner Sam lends Ali the price of a washing machine, to be repaid in six monthly instalments. His bank's statement proves the payment. The notebook records what moved, what was agreed and where, and keeps the proof — and what one owes the other is read from all of it, exactly, never written down beside it.

garden-sam, daftar v0.42.0, the day held at 2026-10-27

What Sam says, and what he reads

Orient — for the person. Sam never opens a terminal. Over six weeks he tells his agent what happened, in his own words, and reads what it did. The conversation is written for this page; every figure, date and refusal the agent reports in it is what the tools print under Operate.

  1. 10 September — a camera.
  2. Sam · spoken

    Ali and I bought a camera. She paid for it — ninety XTS. I use it more, so we said I'd carry two thirds.

  3. Sam's agent

    Written as one agreement between you and Ali, owned by neither of you: 90 XTS on 10 September, paid by Ali, borne two parts by you and one by her. The notebook's check passed, and its journal says what I did. You owe Ali 60 XTS — read from the payment each time you ask, never stored to go stale.

  4. 12 September — a dinner, and a loan.
  5. Sam · spoken

    Ali came to dinner at mine, half past seven. We agreed I'd lend her the money for her washing machine — nothing written, we just said it. I paid the shop 120 the next day. She pays me back 20 on the first of each month, six times; a late one carries one percent a month.

  6. Sam's agent

    Does she start on the first of October?

  7. Sam

    Yes.

  8. Sam's agent

    Two pages. The dinner, as a happening: 12 September 2026 at 19:30, you the host and Ali present — in the Persian calendar, 21 Shahrivar 1405. And the loan: the 120 XTS you paid the shop, with two clauses — 20 XTS on the first of each month, six times from 1 October, and one percent a month on an instalment paid late. Its words were spoken, at that dinner.

  9. A week later — the proof.
  10. Sam · typed

    Here is my card statement for September — the PDF. Keep it, but not my card number.

  11. Sam's agent

    Kept as a document whose identity is its content: if a single byte of it changes, it is another document. I copied the one line that matters — 13 September, the appliance shop, 120.00 XTS — with the card number left out but its last four digits.

    One mistake was caught on the way: I first wrote the file's fingerprint in capital letters, as Windows prints it, and the notebook refused it — it keeps one spelling only, so that two notebooks recognise the same document. Written again, and saved.

  12. 1 October, and after.
  13. Sam · typed

    Ali paid the first 20 today.

  14. Sam's agent

    Recorded, beside the loan — not as a new balance.

  15. Sam · 27 October

    Where do Ali and I stand?

  16. Sam's agent

    You owe her 60 XTS for the camera; she owes you 100 for the loan. Net, Ali owes you 40 XTS. Her next instalment is due on 1 November — in 5 days.

  17. XTS is the code ISO keeps for testing. Your own notebook writes your own currency's code.

What the agent does underneath

Understand — for the curious. Between Sam's words and its answers, the agent calls the notebook's own tools. The same few moves carry the whole story: write a page, say why, save it through the gate, read the answer. Each in plain words first, then exactly, with what the tool printed.

  1. write It writes one page for each thing

    The camera agreement, the dinner, the loan and the statement are four files, each readable on its own.

    Exactly: beans/shared-camera.md and washer-loan.md are contracts, dinner-at-sams.md an event, card-statement-2026-09.md a document anchored by the hash of its bytes — each in the shape of the notebook's recipe, amounts as exact strings. They are shown whole under Inspect.

  2. dmjournal It says what it did

    Every change carries a line in the journal, in the words of whoever made it. The tool reads the time from the clock; nobody types a date.

    ~/garden-sam$ python3 bin/dmjournal.py sam "the camera sam and ali share" --body "- action: added [[shared-camera]]."
## 2026-10-27 09:00+00:00 · sam · the camera sam and ali share
  3. git commit It saves — and the gate checks first

    Saving runs the notebook's gate over every page, not only the new one. If anything breaks a rule, nothing is saved, and the message says what to change.

    ~/garden-sam$ git commit -qm "the camera sam and ali share"
garden-sam (daftar v0.42.0, gardener sam, garden 00479fa7a243): 11 docs, 0 error(s), 0 warning(s)
fast: 14/14 corpus checks passed
    ~/garden-sam$ git commit -qm "the dinner at Sam's"
garden-sam (daftar v0.42.0, gardener sam, garden 00479fa7a243): 10 docs, 0 error(s), 0 warning(s)
…
    ~/garden-sam$ git commit -qm "the washing-machine loan"
garden-sam (daftar v0.42.0, gardener sam, garden 00479fa7a243): 12 docs, 0 error(s), 0 warning(s)
…
  4. git commit A mistake, refused

    The statement's fingerprint in capital letters: refused, nothing saved, and the message names the page, the rule and the form it wants.

    ~/garden-sam-scratch$ git commit -qm "the statement hash, as Get-FileHash prints it"
ERROR card-statement-2026-09: anchor content_hash='sha256:9F86D081884C7D659A2FEAA0C55AD015A3BF4F1B2B0B822CD15D6C15B0F00A08' is not in canonical form (`sha256:` and sixty-four lowercase hexadecimal digits)
      — write it as it was given, in that form. One nobody gave you is left out, never made up: identity: { status: provisional, anchors: [] } (rule content_hash.schema.value_pattern; why: python3 bin/dmwhy.py content_hash)

garden-sam (daftar v0.42.0, gardener sam, garden 00479fa7a243): 13 docs, 1 error(s), 0 warning(s)
exit 1
    ~/garden-sam$ git commit -qm "the September card statement"
garden-sam (daftar v0.42.0, gardener sam, garden 00479fa7a243): 13 docs, 0 error(s), 0 warning(s)
…
  5. dmsafe A payment is added by structure, not by guesswork

    The first instalment goes in beside the loan. The tool edits the page by its structure, and undoes any edit that would lose something by accident.

    ~/garden-sam$ python3 bin/dmsafe.py replace-block beans/washer-loan.md transactions --block ../instalment.yaml
dmsafe: removed nothing; added ['transactions.instalment-1.amount.count', 'transactions.instalment-1.amount.unit', 'transactions.instalment-1.borne_by[0].party', 'transactions.instalment-1.borne_by[0].share', 'transactions.instalment-1.day', 'transactions.instalment-1.paid_by[0].party', 'transactions.instalment-1.under', 'transactions.instalment-1.what']
    ~/garden-sam$ git commit -qm "Ali's first instalment"
garden-sam (daftar v0.42.0, gardener sam, garden 00479fa7a243): 12 docs, 0 error(s), 0 warning(s)
…
  6. dmledger It reads what is owed — never a stored balance

    Each bearer owes each payer its share of what that payer paid, in fractions, so nothing is rounded.

    ~/garden-sam$ python3 bin/dmledger.py shared-camera
…
   sam owes ali 60 XTS
    ~/garden-sam$ python3 bin/dmledger.py --between sam ali
== between sam and ali
   shared-camera: sam owes ali 60 XTS
   washer-loan: ali owes sam 100 XTS
   net, across 2 agreements:
   ali owes sam 40 XTS
  7. dmstale and dmcal It reads what falls due, in any calendar

    Dates are read on every check, not remembered; and a day is one day whichever calendar names it.

    ~/garden-sam$ python3 bin/dmstale.py
EXPIRING washer-loan.clauses[instalments]  due 2026-11-01 (5 days)  — next: occurrence 2 of 6, each month in gregorian-civil, 6 times  <-- a clause falls due, and from that day the party it is owed to is owed it
…
exit 1
    ~/garden-sam$ python3 bin/dmcal.py 2026-09-12

What the tools say

Operate — for the keeper. The same tools, whole: what anyone who keeps this notebook sees when they run them, and what they do when one refuses.

A camera, its cost shared

dmledger reads the transaction. Ali paid 90 XTS and bears one part in three; each bearer owes each payer its share of what that payer paid:

~/garden-sam$ python3 bin/dmledger.py shared-camera
== shared-camera — shared-camera — Sam and Ali bought a camera together
   parties: ali, sam
   transactions (1):
     camera  2026-09-10  90 XTS  paid by ali  · borne by ali 30 XTS, sam 60 XTS  "the camera, bought online"
   XTS  position (paid less borne): ali +60 XTS · sam -60 XTS
   sam owes ali 60 XTS

Had the camera cost 100.00 XTS, a third would not come out even in XTS's two decimal places. In a throwaway clone of the garden, dmsafe changes the one count, and dmledger reads it again:

~$ git clone -q ~/garden-sam ~/garden-sam-scratch
~/garden-sam-scratch$ python3 bin/dmsafe.py flow-set beans/shared-camera.md transactions.camera.amount.count '"100.00"' --expect 1
dmsafe: removed nothing; added nothing
~/garden-sam-scratch$ python3 bin/dmledger.py shared-camera
== shared-camera — shared-camera — Sam and Ali bought a camera together
   parties: ali, sam
   transactions (1):
     camera  2026-09-10  100 XTS  paid by ali  · borne by ali 100/3 XTS, sam 200/3 XTS  "the camera, bought online"
       NOTE 200/3, 100/3 XTS (does not come out even in XTS's 2 places — who takes the remainder is a clause)
   XTS  position (paid less borne): ali +200/3 XTS · sam -200/3 XTS
   sam owes ali 200/3 XTS (does not come out even in XTS's 2 places — who takes the remainder is a clause)
A dinner, where the loan was agreed

dmcal gives that day in every calendar it knows. Every calendar here meets the others at the day; a calendar that is not reckoned by rule is said to be so, and its date is not guessed:

A loan, repaid in instalments

On 2026-10-27, the day these pages are held at, Ali owes the whole loan, and the next instalment falls due in 5 days:

~/garden-sam$ python3 bin/dmledger.py washer-loan
== washer-loan — washer-loan — Sam lent Ali the price of a washing machine, repaid monthly
   parties: ali, sam
   transactions (1):
     the-loan  2026-09-13  120 XTS  paid by sam  · borne by ali 120 XTS  "Sam paid the shop for Ali's washing machine"
   XTS  position (paid less borne): ali -120 XTS · sam +120 XTS
   ali owes sam 120 XTS
   clauses in force (2):
    instalments  "Ali repays 20 XTS on the first day of each month, six times"
        by ali · to sam · required · amount 20 XTS · due 2026-10-01, then each month in gregorian-civil, 6 times — next 2026-11-01 (in 5 days), occurrence 2 of 6
    late-interest  "an instalment paid after its day carries one percent of itself for each month it is late"
        by ali · to sam · required · amount 1 percent · in force when: "an instalment is paid late"

Ali pays her first instalment. It is a transaction too — Ali paid it, Sam bears it, under: instalments. The build writes the whole transactions block with the new entry in it:

input: instalment.yamltransactions:
  the-loan:
    what: "Sam paid the shop for Ali's washing machine"
    amount: { count: "120.00", unit: XTS }
    day: 2026-09-13
    paid_by:
      - { party: sam }
    borne_by:
      - { party: ali, share: 1 }
  instalment-1:
    what: "Ali's first instalment"
    amount: { count: "20.00", unit: XTS }
    day: 2026-10-01
    under: instalments
    paid_by:
      - { party: ali }
    borne_by:
      - { party: sam, share: 1 }

dmsafe replaces the block by its key, and says which leaf paths it removed and which it added:

The statement that proves it, its secret left out

The cookbook gives the command that prints the hash on each system:

content_hash is sha256: and the file's SHA-256 in lowercase hexadecimal: sha256sum <file> prints it on Linux, shasum -a 256 <file> on macOS, and in PowerShell "sha256:" + (Get-FileHash -Algorithm SHA256 <file>).Hash.ToLower() — Get-FileHash prints capitals, and the gate refuses them.

In a throwaway clone of the garden, the build writes the hash in capitals, as Get-FileHash prints it, in a block for dmsafe:

input: anchor.yamlidentity:
  status: confirmed
  anchors:
    - { key: content_hash, value: "sha256:9F86D081884C7D659A2FEAA0C55AD015A3BF4F1B2B0B822CD15D6C15B0F00A08", class: logical, establishing: true }

dmsafe finds nothing lost — the value changed, and no leaf path went — and the gate refuses the commit:

~/garden-sam-scratch$ python3 bin/dmsafe.py replace-block beans/card-statement-2026-09.md identity --block ../anchor.yaml
dmsafe: removed nothing; added nothing
~/garden-sam-scratch$ python3 bin/dmjournal.py sam "the statement hash, as Get-FileHash prints it" --body "- action: [[card-statement-2026-09]] content_hash in capitals."
## 2026-10-27 09:00+00:00 · sam · the statement hash, as Get-FileHash prints it
~/garden-sam-scratch$ git add -A
~/garden-sam-scratch$ git commit -qm "the statement hash, as Get-FileHash prints it"
ERROR card-statement-2026-09: anchor content_hash='sha256:9F86D081884C7D659A2FEAA0C55AD015A3BF4F1B2B0B822CD15D6C15B0F00A08' is not in canonical form (`sha256:` and sixty-four lowercase hexadecimal digits)
      — write it as it was given, in that form. One nobody gave you is left out, never made up: identity: { status: provisional, anchors: [] } (rule content_hash.schema.value_pattern; why: python3 bin/dmwhy.py content_hash)

garden-sam (daftar v0.42.0, gardener sam, garden 00479fa7a243): 13 docs, 1 error(s), 0 warning(s)
exit 1

What the notebook records

Inspect — for the auditor. Exactly what was written, and why it is shaped so.

A camera, its cost shared

An agreement is a contract bean. This one is owned by neither of them: it ends at the crown, where every chain of ownership ends, in its branch logos — the word, for what exists by being said and agreed — and both parties answer for it. A party with accepted said yes on that day. The transaction says what moved: an amount, the day, who paid, and who bears it in whole-number shares.

beans/shared-camera.md---
bean: shared-camera
genos: contract
title: "shared-camera — Sam and Ali bought a camera together"
status: active
summary: "Sam and Ali share a camera; Ali paid for it, and they bear its cost two to one."
nature: lekton
identity:
  status: confirmed
  anchors:
    - { key: identifier, value: "contract:shared-camera", class: logical, establishing: true }
provenance: { src: asserted-by-human, by: "sam", as_of: 2026-10-27 }
owned_by: { legal: { crown: logos } }
responsibility: { legal: { parties: true } }
parties:
  sam: { who: { bean: sam }, accepted: 2026-09-10 }
  ali: { who: { bean: ali }, accepted: 2026-09-10 }
over:
  - { what: "a camera the two of them use" }
words: { form: spoken, agreed: 2026-09-10 }
transactions:
  camera:
    what: "the camera, bought online"
    amount: { count: "90.00", unit: XTS }
    day: 2026-09-10
    paid_by:
      - { party: ali }
    borne_by:
      - { party: sam, share: 2 }
      - { party: ali, share: 1 }
---
Sam uses the camera more, so Sam bears two parts of its cost and Ali one.

An amount is exact: a count written as a string, with no more places than the currency uses, and never a float. XTS is the code ISO reserves for testing; a garden writes its own currency's.

A dinner, where the loan was agreed

A happening between people is owned by none of them: it ends at the crown, in its branch logos — the word, for what exists by being said and agreed — as an agreement may, and whoever hosted it answers for it. When it began is timing, at the resolution actually known — a minute, with its offset. Who took part is refs, each naming what they were:

beans/dinner-at-sams.md---
bean: dinner-at-sams
genos: event
title: "dinner-at-sams — dinner at Sam's, where the washing-machine loan was agreed"
status: active
summary: "Ali came to dinner at Sam's; they agreed the loan for her washing machine."
nature: lekton
identity:
  status: confirmed
  anchors:
    - { key: identifier, value: "event:dinner-at-sams-2026-09-12", class: logical, establishing: true }
provenance: { src: asserted-by-human, by: "sam", as_of: 2026-10-27 }
owned_by: { legal: { crown: logos } }
responsibility: { legal: { holder: { bean: sam } } }
timing:
  start: { system: gregorian-civil, at: "2026-09-12 19:30+03:00", unit: minute }
refs:
  host:  { bean: sam, rel: host }
  guest: { bean: ali, rel: present }
---
Dinner at Sam's.

The loan points at it. Its words were spoken, at this event, on that day:

beans/washer-loan.md…
words: { form: spoken, at: { bean: dinner-at-sams }, agreed: 2026-09-12 }
…
A loan, repaid in instalments

Each clause says who it binds, whom it is owed to, how much, from when, and how it repeats; unless it says otherwise (stance), it is required. The late interest is not brought into force by a date, so it says what brings it: when. The words were spoken, at the dinner:

beans/washer-loan.md---
bean: washer-loan
genos: contract
title: "washer-loan — Sam lent Ali the price of a washing machine, repaid monthly"
status: active
summary: "Sam paid for Ali's washing machine; Ali repays it in six monthly instalments, with interest on one paid late."
nature: lekton
identity:
  status: confirmed
  anchors:
    - { key: identifier, value: "contract:washer-loan", class: logical, establishing: true }
provenance: { src: asserted-by-human, by: "sam", as_of: 2026-10-27 }
owned_by: { legal: { crown: logos } }
responsibility: { legal: { parties: true } }
parties:
  sam: { who: { bean: sam }, role: lender, accepted: 2026-09-12 }
  ali: { who: { bean: ali }, role: borrower, accepted: 2026-09-12 }
over:
  - { what: "the price of Ali's washing machine" }
words: { form: spoken, at: { bean: dinner-at-sams }, agreed: 2026-09-12 }
clauses:
  instalments:
    what: "Ali repays 20 XTS on the first day of each month, six times"
    by: ali
    to: sam
    amount: { count: "20.00", unit: XTS }
    due: 2026-10-01
    every: { of: time, in: gregorian-civil, each: month, times: 6 }
  late-interest:
    what: "an instalment paid after its day carries one percent of itself for each month it is late"
    by: ali
    to: sam
    amount: { count: 1, unit: percent }
    when: { said: "an instalment is paid late" }
transactions:
  the-loan:
    what: "Sam paid the shop for Ali's washing machine"
    amount: { count: "120.00", unit: XTS }
    day: 2026-09-13
    paid_by:
      - { party: sam }
    borne_by:
      - { party: ali, share: 1 }
---
Agreed over dinner; nothing was written down.
The statement that proves it, its secret left out

The bank wrote the statement, so the bank owns it; Sam holds the copy, and located_at says where it is. content_hash is the SHA-256 of the file's bytes. The lines are a capture: never authoritative, keyed by what would make it stale, and carrying no secret — redactions says what was left out, and why. taken_at is the moment it was taken, in milliseconds.

beans/card-statement-2026-09.md---
bean: card-statement-2026-09
genos: document
title: "card-statement-2026-09 — Sam's card statement for September 2026"
status: active
summary: "The statement Sam's bank issued for the card that paid for the washing machine, kept as a PDF."
nature: lekton
identity:
  status: confirmed
  anchors:
    - { key: content_hash, value: "sha256:9f86d081884c7d659a2feaa0c55ad015a3bf4f1b2b0b822cd15d6c15b0f00a08", class: logical, establishing: true }
provenance: { src: observed, by: "sam", as_of: 2026-10-27 }
owned_by: { legal: { external: "the bank that issued the card, which wrote the statement" } }
responsibility: { legal: { holder: { bean: sam } } }
located_at:
  - { system: unix-filesystem, openness: here, at: "laptop:/home/user/documents/card-statement-2026-09.pdf", observed: 2026-10-27 }
capture:
  lines:
    of: "the statement's transaction lines for September 2026"
    owned_by_them: "the bank that issued the card"
    source: "read by hand from the PDF, page 1"
    taken_at: 1790928000000
    staleness_key: "the statement's content_hash: a changed byte is another statement"
    redactions: "the card number, all but its last four digits — a card number is a secret"
    holds: "2026-09-13 · appliance shop · 120.00 XTS · card ending 4242"
---
The card statement.

What it shows

  • What is owed is read, never written. There is no balance to store: a stored balance is a second copy of what the transactions say, and it drifts the first time a transaction is added and the balance is not.
  • Nothing is rounded. Every share is a fraction. One that does not come out even is shown as the fraction it is, and who takes the remainder is something the parties agree, in a clause.
  • No currency is privileged. Every amount stays in the currency it was paid in; no factor joins two currencies, and a rate is an observation someone made. No privileged sibling.
  • An agreement between two people may be owned by neither. Then both answer for it, and the two notebooks' records of it agree about its owner. The crown.
  • An agreement's words are recorded as they stand: written in a document, spoken at an event, or not yet put into words.
  • No calendar is privileged. A moment is written in the calendar it was known in, and two calendars are compared through the day they both fall on. No privileged sibling.
  • A clause says what became of it. Its state — met, waived, broken, disputed — is written the day something becomes of it, and dmstale stops warning once it says met, waived or broken. Declared whole.
  • A repetition names its calendar. “Each month” means nothing until it says whose month: here gregorian-civil. No privileged sibling.
  • A document is identified by its bytes. A copy anywhere with the same hash is the same statement; one changed byte is another.
  • A capture is a copy, knowingly taken. A fact is authoritative here, a pointer to whoever owns it, or a capture: dated, keyed by what would make it stale, never applied back without reading the source again, and never holding a secret.

Read more

  • The cookbook: money between two people.
  • The model: agreements and money.
  • The tool: bin/dmledger.py, explained in its first lines.
  • The cookbook: a happening: an event.
  • The reasoning: python3 bin/dmwhy.py 'anchor_systems[gregorian-civil]' — why no calendar is privileged.
  • The tool: bin/dmcal.py.
  • The cookbook: an agreement paid in instalments.
  • The cookbook: a statement: a document, and a capture of its lines, with the statement's copy on Windows.
  • The model: ground rule 3, reference external truth or capture it knowingly.

Is your situation like this, but not quite? Tell us how it differs.

Two people's notebooks meet

Ali keeps a notebook of her own, and nothing outside it writes there — not Sam, and not Sam's agent. Sam's garden lays a proposal beside both, under an agreement both are parties to. Ali's garden reads it, takes it into the working tree, and her commit is what lets it in. Taking the agreement records what Sam's garden offers; her yes is hers to write.

garden-sam and garden-ali, daftar v0.42.0, the day held at 2026-10-27

What Sam and Ali say, and what they read

Orient — for the two people. Each talks to their own agent, in their own notebook. Neither agent writes in the other's. The conversations are written for this page; what the agents report is what the tools print under Operate.

  1. In Sam's notebook.
  2. Sam · typed

    Send Ali the camera agreement, so her notebook has it too.

  3. Sam's agent

    Her notebook is hers: I cannot write in it, and nothing should. I have made a proposal instead — one file, laid beside both notebooks, offering the camera agreement under the agreement itself, which you are both party to. The file carries a fingerprint, so her side can tell if a line of it was changed.

    Nothing has been sent. Give her the file however you like; your journal already records the file, who it went to, and the agreement it went under.

  4. In Ali's notebook.
  5. Ali

    Sam gave me a file from his notebook. What is it?

  6. Ali's agent

    A proposal from Sam's notebook, made on 27 October. Its fingerprint checks: nobody changed a line of it. Both notebooks keep the same law, so it can be read here. It offers the camera — 90 XTS you paid, borne two parts by Sam and one by you. But your notebook has not met Sam's yet. May I write him in, and his notebook, first?

  7. Ali

    Yes.

  8. Ali's agent

    Done, and read again: it passes your notebook's check. I have taken it in — your notebook now holds the camera agreement under the name Sam's gave it, and reads the same debt: Sam owes you 60 XTS.

    Taking it in accepted nothing for you. It says you agreed, but that is Sam's record. Do you agree?

  9. Ali

    I do.

  10. Ali's agent

    Written: your own yes, in your own words and your own commit. If the same file comes again, it will be refused: it was taken already.

  11. Before the loan crosses — trying it first.
  12. Sam

    Before the washing-machine loan goes to Ali for real, can we try it?

  13. Sam's agent

    I have grown a rehearsal notebook — a notebook of its own that says, in its own first page, that it is a rehearsal — and made the proposal from there. Ali's side can read it and see exactly what would happen.

  14. Ali's agent

    This one comes from a rehearsal. It reads cleanly, and it would pass your notebook's check — but your notebook will not take it in as fact: a rehearsal never passes for anyone's word. I can take it into a scratch copy, marked as a rehearsal, if you want to see it there.

What the agents do underneath

Understand — for the curious. Two agents, two notebooks, one file between them. Each step in plain words, with the lines of the tool's output that carry it.

  1. write Sam's agent writes Ali, and her notebook, in his

    Before anything can be offered to Ali's notebook, Sam's has to know it: Ali as a person, and her notebook by its id — the name her own notebook gives it.

    ~/garden-sam$ python3 bin/dmjournal.py sam "first contact: garden-ali and ali" --body "- action: first contact: [[garden-ali]] and [[ali]], by the id ali read out (class F, sam ratified); [[ali-consent]], what ali agreed sam may keep."
## 2026-10-27 09:00+00:00 · sam · first contact: garden-ali and ali
~/garden-sam$ git commit -qm "first contact: garden-ali and ali"
garden-sam (daftar v0.42.0, gardener sam, garden 00479fa7a243): 9 docs, 0 error(s), 0 warning(s)
fast: 14/14 corpus checks passed
  2. dmpropose mint Sam's agent gives the agreement a name both can use

    Before anything crosses, the agreement's name is qualified by the notebook that recorded it first. Choosing a name is the gardener's decision, so the tool prints the command instead of running it.

    ~/garden-sam$ python3 bin/dmpropose.py mint shared-camera
…
  python3 bin/dmsafe.py flow-set beans/shared-camera.md "identity.anchors[value=contract:shared-camera].value" "'00479fa7a243/contract:shared-camera'" --expect 1
Nothing was written. An anchor is the gardener's to choose (class F): after the edit, journal it and commit.
  3. dmpropose make It writes one file, outside both notebooks

    The proposal is a single file with a fingerprint. Nothing is committed and nothing is sent.

What the notebooks record

Inspect — for the auditor. Exactly what was written, and why it is shaped so.

A garden is known by the commit it grew from. Each gardener runs dmpropose id and tells the other what it prints:

~/garden-sam$ python3 bin/dmpropose.py id
garden_id: "00479fa7a243"
garden: garden-sam
gardener: sam
~/garden-ali$ python3 bin/dmpropose.py id
garden_id: "bdaf9c70e051"
garden: garden-ali
gardener: ali

First contact, on Sam's side: a garden bean anchored by the id Ali read out, owned by her and answered for by her — so Ali is a person bean here too, named as her own garden names her. No bean owns a person: hers ends at the crown's branch agape, love that does not possess, and she answers for herself (the words). The two are one commit, and the gardener's decision:

beans/garden-ali.md---
bean: garden-ali
genos: garden
title: "garden-ali — the garden Ali keeps"
status: active
summary: "Ali's own daftar garden. She keeps it; what passes between it and this one is proposed, never written."
nature: lekton
identity:
  status: confirmed
  anchors:
    - { key: garden_id, value: "bdaf9c70e051", class: logical, establishing: true }
provenance: { src: asserted-by-human, by: "sam", as_of: 2026-10-27 }
owned_by: { legal: { owner: { bean: ali } } }
responsibility: { legal: { holder: { bean: ali } } }
---
Ali's garden. Its id is what `python3 bin/dmpropose.py id` printed there.
beans/ali.md---
bean: ali
genos: person
title: "Ali"
status: active
summary: "Ali, who keeps a garden of her own; Sam shares costs with her."
nature: empsychon

  status: confirmed
  anchors:
    - { key: identifier, value: "bdaf9c70e051/person:ali", class: logical, establishing: true }
provenance: { src: asserted-by-human, by: "sam", as_of: 2026-10-27 }
consent: { bean: ali-consent }
owned_by: { legal: { crown: agape } }
responsibility: { legal: { self: true } }
---
Ali keeps garden-ali. Her name here is the one her own garden gave her.

The camera they share is the agreement the proposal is made under; it is on the cost shared page.

The rehearsal

The rehearsal is grown by germination, never cloned: a clone is the same garden, with the same id, and what it proposes is that garden's word. Its GARDEN.md says what it rehearses:

GARDEN.md---
garden: garden-sam-rehearsal
extends: std-vocab@26.0
daftar_release: "v0.42.0"  # the daftar release this garden runs; bin/dmupgrade.py moves it
gardener: sam                      # who keeps this garden: its first bean
test: "a rehearsal of the washer loan"
…

Ali records the rehearsal garden as she records any garden, and marks it test — her own record, whatever its proposals say:

beans/garden-sam-rehearsal.md---
bean: garden-sam-rehearsal
genos: garden
title: "garden-sam-rehearsal — the garden sam keeps"
status: active
summary: "Another garden this one deals with."
nature: lekton
owned_by: { legal: { owner: { bean: sam } } }
responsibility: { legal: { holder: { bean: sam } } }
identity:
  status: confirmed
  anchors:
    - { key: garden_id, value: "bf94a88a0be9", class: logical, establishing: true }
provenance: { src: asserted-by-human, by: "ali (gardener)", as_of: 2026-10-27 }
test: "a rehearsal of the washer loan"
---
The garden sam keeps; its id is the one its proposal garden-sam-rehearsal-20261027-0900 carried.

What it shows

  • Gardens meet only by proposal. What passes is one file, never a write; reading it writes nothing, taking it commits nothing, and the gardener's commit is the ratification. Peering.
  • Taking is not accepting. Taking an agreement records what the other garden offers. A party's acceptance is its own word, written by its own garden.
  • A name is minted once, and carried. The garden that recorded the camera first names it, and Ali's garden keeps the name byte for byte, so the two notebooks see one agreement, not two.
  • Consent comes first. A proposal is made under an agreement both gardeners are parties to.
  • A rehearsal is grown, never copied. A clone of a real garden carries its id, and what it proposed would be that garden's word.
  • Each gardener marks it in their own records. The rehearsal says so in its manifest; the receiving garden says so on its own garden bean, and holds later proposals to it whatever they say.
  • A rehearsal never passes for a person's word. A garden that is not a test garden takes one in only with --as-test, and then every bean it writes says so.

Read more

  • The cookbook: another person, and the garden she keeps; proposing to another garden.
  • The model: between gardens: the mycelium; MERGE.md §16, how a proposal is read and taken.
  • The tool: bin/dmpropose.py.
  • The cookbook: proposing to another garden, its last part, a rehearsal.
  • The model: between gardens, a test garden.

Is your situation like this, but not quite? Tell us how it differs.

Several agents, one notebook

Agents of different makes work in one garden, one after another, with none of each other's context. What keeps their work in one language is not their memory but the gate: every write passes it or is refused, whoever made it. An assistant in a chat window, with no shell, cannot run the gate, so what it gives is a proposal, and the gardener commits it or does not.

garden-sam, daftar v0.42.0, the day held at 2026-10-27

What Sam says, and what he reads

Orient — for the person. Sam works with more than one agent: a coding agent that can run the notebook's tools, and an assistant in a chat window that cannot. They share nothing but the notebook. The conversations are written for this page; what the agents report is what the tools print under Operate.

  1. In a chat window, on the phone.
  2. Sam

    Add the office printer to my notebook. The serial on the label is PRN-7781.

  3. The chat assistant

    I cannot run your notebook's gate from here, so I cannot add it myself. Here is a proposal instead: the printer's page and the journal line that would go with it. Give it to an agent that can run the gate. One thing I could not check: whether your notebook accepts the serial as written on the label.

  4. Later, with the coding agent.
  5. Sam

    My chat assistant wrote this for the notebook. Take it in.

  6. Sam's agent

    Read. It comes from a chat, so there is no fingerprint to check it against: you vouch for where it came from. I tried it on a scratch copy of the notebook first — it passes. Taken in: the printer's page is written, and the journal quotes the assistant's own words as what they are — its words, not instructions to me.

    Nothing is saved until you say so: your yes is the commit.

  7. Sam

    Yes, save it.

  8. Sam's agent

    Saved. The record says who proposed it — the chat assistant — and who enacted it — you.

  9. The same holds for any agent: a small model running on your own computer, too. It has no memory of what the others did; it reads the notebook, and the gate judges its work exactly as it judges theirs.

What the agents do underneath

Understand — for the curious. What keeps several agents' work in one language is not their memory but the gate. Each step in plain words, with the lines of the tool's output that carry it.

  1. dmcheck An agent arriving checks where it stands

    The first thing any agent runs. Its last line names the notebook and its gardener: if it runs, the agent can write; if not, it can only propose.

    ~/garden-sam$ python3 bin/dmcheck.py --all

    A change without its journal line is not saved, whoever made it — and the message gives the exact command that fixes it.

    ~/garden-sam$ git add beans/printer.md

What the tools say

Operate — for the keeper. The same tools, whole: what anyone who keeps this notebook sees when they run them, and what they do when one refuses.

A writer, and the gate

The first thing an agent runs, and shows the person it works for. If it runs, the agent is a writer:

~/garden-sam$ python3 bin/dmcheck.py --all
garden-sam (daftar v0.42.0, gardener sam, garden 00479fa7a243): 13 docs, 0 error(s), 0 warning(s)

A writer stages a bean for the office printer and commits it with no journal entry. The gate refuses, and says the line to write:

~/garden-sam$ git add beans/printer.md
~/garden-sam$ git commit -qm "the office printer"
ERROR state-change staged (beans/printer.md…) but log/journal.md not updated
      — provenance duty. Append an entry naming what changed and why. Its heading is read from the clock by the tool, never typed; this writes it, stages everything and commits:
      python3 bin/dmsave.py "<who>" "<what>" --body "- action: <what was done to beans/printer.md>"

garden-sam (daftar v0.42.0, gardener sam, garden 00479fa7a243): 14 docs, 1 error(s), 0 warning(s)
exit 1
An assistant with no shell, and its proposal

An assistant in a chat gives the same bean as a proposal, in the shape WELCOME.md shows: front matter saying what it is and whom it is from, the journal entry's lines, the bean whole, and what it could not check, outside every fence:

input: chat-20261027-0900.md---
proposal: chat-20261027-0900
from: { name: "an assistant in a chat, for sam" }
beans: [printer]

- action: added [[printer]], proposed by an assistant in chat.
- detail: serial as read by sam from the label; everything else as sam described it.
- why: the printer was the one networked device not yet in the ledger.
